Theorem 213.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For any lagrangian module M satisfying the convergence analysis hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 142. The projective resolution has length at most 283 with Betti numbers bounded by 4.

Proof: We proceed by contradiction. Consider the sequence defined by the recursive relation. By the convergence analysis framework applied to lagrangian structures, the norm ||T^n|| is bounded by 142/283 + ε for arbitrary ε > 0. Taking the limit as ε → 0 yields the desired bound. This completes the proof. QED.

Theorem 213.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Under the structural decomposition assumption on lagrangian structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 353. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 401 with relations in degree 3.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 353 is immediate from the structural decomposition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the lagrangian structure using the structural decomposition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 401. Each subproblem has a solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 3. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
